That will make your server run much faster, and will\\n7: also prevent malicious users from seeing details of your application that can\\n8: be revealed by the error pages.\\n9: \\n10: However, running with :setting:`DEBUG` set to ``False`` means you'll never see\\n11: errors generated by your site -- everyone will instead see your public error\\n12: pages. You need to keep track of errors that occur in deployed sites, so Django\\n13: can be configured to create reports with details about those errors.\\n14: \\n15: Email reports\\n16: =============\\n17: \\n18: Server errors\\n19: -------------\\n20: \\n21: When :setting:`DEBUG` is ``False``, Django will email the users listed in the\\n22: :setting:`ADMINS` setting whenever your code raises an unhandled exception and\\n23: results in an internal server error (strictly speaking, for any response with\\n24: an HTTP status code of 500 or greater). This gives the administrators immediate\\n25: notification of any errors. The :setting:`ADMINS` will get a description of the\\n26: error, a complete Python traceback, and details about the HTTP request that\\n27: caused the error.\\n28: \\n29: .. note::\\n30: \\n31:    In order to send email, Django requires a few settings telling it\\n32:    how to connect to your mail server. At the very least, you'll need\\n33:    to specify :setting:`EMAIL_HOST` and possibly\\n34:    :setting:`EMAIL_HOST_USER` and :setting:`EMAIL_HOST_PASSWORD`,\\n35:    though other settings may be also required depending on your mail\\n36:    server's configuration. Consult :doc:`the Django settings\\n37:    documentation </ref/settings>` for a full list of email-related\\n38:    settings.\\n39: \\n40: By default, Django will send email from ``root@localhost``. However, some mail\\n41: providers reject all email from this address. To use a different sender\\n42: address, modify the :setting:`SERVER_EMAIL` setting.\\n43: \\n44: To activate this behavior, put the email addresses of the recipients in the\\n45: :setting:`ADMINS` setting.\\n46: \\n47: .. seealso::\\n48: \\n49:     Server error emails are sent using the logging framework, so you can\\n50:     customize this behavior by :doc:`customizing your logging configuration\\n51:     </topics/logging>`.\\n52: \\n53: 404 errors\\n54: ----------\\n55: \\n56: Django can also be configured to email errors about broken links (404 \\\"page\\n57: not found\\\" errors). That will make your server run much faster, and will\\n7: also prevent malicious users from seeing details of your application that can\\n8: be revealed by the error pages.\\n9: \\n10: However, running with :setting:`DEBUG` set to ``False`` means you'll never see\\n11: errors generated by your site -- everyone will instead see your public error\\n12: pages. You need to keep track of errors that occur in deployed sites, so Django\\n13: can be configured to create reports with details about those errors.\\n14: \\n15: Email reports\\n16: =============\\n17: \\n18: Server errors\\n19: -------------\\n20: \\n21: When :setting:`DEBUG` is ``False``, Django will email the users listed in the\\n22: :setting:`ADMINS` setting whenever your code raises an unhandled exception and\\n23: results in an internal server error (strictly speaking, for any response with\\n24: an HTTP status code of 500 or greater). This gives the administrators immediate\\n25: notification of any errors. The :setting:`ADMINS` will get a description of the\\n26: error, a complete Python traceback, and details about the HTTP request that\\n27: caused the error.\\n28: \\n29: .. note::\\n30: \\n31:    In order to send email, Django requires a few settings telling it\\n32:    how to connect to your mail server. At the very least, you'll need\\n33:    to specify :setting:`EMAIL_HOST` and possibly\\n34:    :setting:`EMAIL_HOST_USER` and :setting:`EMAIL_HOST_PASSWORD`,\\n35:    though other settings may be also required depending on your mail\\n36:    server's configuration. Consult :doc:`the Django settings\\n37:    documentation </ref/settings>` for a full list of email-related\\n38:    settings.\\n39: \\n40: By default, Django will send email from ``root@localhost``. However, some mail\\n41: providers reject all email from this address. To use a different sender\\n42: address, modify the :setting:`SERVER_EMAIL` setting.\\n43: \\n44: To activate this behavior, put the email addresses of the recipients in the\\n45: :setting:`ADMINS` setting.\\n46: \\n47: .. seealso::\\n48: \\n49:     Server error emails are sent using the logging framework, so you can\\n50:     customize this behavior by :doc:`customizing your logging configuration\\n51:     </topics/logging>`.\\n52: \\n53: 404 errors\\n54: ----------\\n55: \\n56: Django can also be configured to email errors about broken links (404 \\\"page\\n57: not found\\\" errors). Django sends emails about 404 errors when:\\n58: \\n59: * :setting:`DEBUG` is ``False``;\\n60: \\n61: * Your :setting:`MIDDLEWARE` setting includes\\n62:   :class:`django.middleware.common.BrokenLinkEmailsMiddleware`.\\n63: \\n64: If those conditions are met, Django will email the users listed in the\\n65: :setting:`MANAGERS` setting whenever your code raises a 404 and the request has\\n66: a referer. It doesn't bother to email for 404s that don't have a referer --\\n67: those are usually people typing in broken URLs or broken web bots. It also\\n68: ignores 404s when the referer is equal to the requested URL, since this\\n69: behavior is from broken web bots too.\\n70: \\n71: \",\"url\":\"https://sourcegraph.sourcegraph.com/github.com/django/django@7c54fee7760b1c61fd7f9cb7cc6a2965f4236137/-/blob/docs/howto/error-reporting.txt%3FL1-70\"}","structuredContent":{"content":"1: =============================\n2: How to manage error reporting\n3: =============================\n4: \n5: When you're running a public site you should always turn off the\n6: :setting:`DEBUG` setting. That will make your server run much faster, and will\n7: also prevent malicious users from seeing details of your application that can\n8: be revealed by the error pages.\n9: \n10: However, running with :setting:`DEBUG` set to ``False`` means you'll never see\n11: errors generated by your site -- everyone will instead see your public error\n12: pages. You need to keep track of errors that occur in deployed sites, so Django\n13: can be configured to create reports with details about those errors.\n14: \n15: Email reports\n16: =============\n17: \n18: Server errors\n19: -------------\n20: \n21: When :setting:`DEBUG` is ``False``, Django will email the users listed in the\n22: :setting:`ADMINS` setting whenever your code raises an unhandled exception and\n23: results in an internal server error (strictly speaking, for any response with\n24: an HTTP status code of 500 or greater). This gives the administrators immediate\n25: notification of any errors. The :setting:`ADMINS` will get a description of the\n26: error, a complete Python traceback, and details about the HTTP request that\n27: caused the error.\n28: \n29: .. note::\n30: \n31:    In order to send email, Django requires a few settings telling it\n32:    how to connect to your mail server. At the very least, you'll need\n33:    to specify :setting:`EMAIL_HOST` and possibly\n34:    :setting:`EMAIL_HOST_USER` and :setting:`EMAIL_HOST_PASSWORD`,\n35:    though other settings may be also required depending on your mail\n36:    server's configuration. Consult :doc:`the Django settings\n37:    documentation </ref/settings>` for a full list of email-related\n38:    settings.\n39: \n40: By default, Django will send email from ``root@localhost``. However, some mail\n41: providers reject all email from this address. To use a different sender\n42: address, modify the :setting:`SERVER_EMAIL` setting.\n43: \n44: To activate this behavior, put the email addresses of the recipients in the\n45: :setting:`ADMINS` setting.\n46: \n47: .. seealso::\n48: \n49:     Server error emails are sent using the logging framework, so you can\n50:     customize this behavior by :doc:`customizing your logging configuration\n51:     </topics/logging>`.\n52: \n53: 404 errors\n54: ----------\n55: \n56: Django can also be configured to email errors about broken links (404 \"page\n57: not found\" errors). Django sends emails about 404 errors when:\n58: \n59: * :setting:`DEBUG` is ``False``;\n60: \n61: * Your :setting:`MIDDLEWARE` setting includes\n62:   :class:`django.middleware.common.BrokenLinkEmailsMiddleware`.\n63: \n64: If those conditions are met, Django will email the users listed in the\n65: :setting:`MANAGERS` setting whenever your code raises a 404 and the request has\n66: a referer. It doesn't bother to email for 404s that don't have a referer --\n67: those are usually people typing in broken URLs or broken web bots. It also\n68: ignores 404s when the referer is equal to the requested URL, since this\n69: behavior is from broken web bots too.\n70: \n71: ","url":"https://sourcegraph.sourcegraph.com/github.com/django/django@7c54fee7760b1c61fd7f9cb7cc6a2965f4236137/-/blob/docs/howto/error-reporting.txt%3FL1-70"}}}

The email subject will\\n325:     include the phrase \\\"internal IP\\\" if the client's IP address is in the\\n326:     :setting:`INTERNAL_IPS` setting; if not, it will include \\\"EXTERNAL IP\\\".\\n327: \\n328:     If the log record contains stack trace information, that stack\\n329:     trace will be included in the email.\\n330: \\n331:     The ``include_html`` argument of ``AdminEmailHandler`` is used to\\n332:     control whether the traceback email includes an HTML attachment\\n333:     containing the full content of the debug web page that would have been\\n334:     produced if :setting:`DEBUG` were ``True``. To set this value in your\\n335:     configuration, include it in the handler definition for\\n336:     ``django.utils.log.AdminEmailHandler``, like this::\\n337: \\n338:         \\\"handlers\\\": {\\n339:             \\\"mail_admins\\\": {\\n340:                 \\\"level\\\": \\\"ERROR\\\",\\n341:                 \\\"class\\\": \\\"django.utils.log.AdminEmailHandler\\\",\\n342:                 \\\"include_html\\\": True,\\n343:             },\\n344:         }\\n345: \\n346:     Be aware of the :ref:`security implications of logging\\n347:     <logging-security-implications>` when using the ``AdminEmailHandler``.\\n348: \\n349:     By setting the ``email_backend`` argument of ``AdminEmailHandler``, the\\n350:     :ref:`email backend <topic-email-backends>` that is being used by the\\n351:     handler can be overridden, like this::\\n352: \\n353:         \\\"handlers\\\": {\\n354:             \\\"mail_admins\\\": {\\n355:                 \\\"level\\\": \\\"ERROR\\\",\\n356:                 \\\"class\\\": \\\"django.utils.log.AdminEmailHandler\\\",\\n357:                 \\\"email_backend\\\": \\\"django.core.mail.backends.filebased.EmailBackend\\\",\\n358:             },\\n359:         }\\n360: \\n361:     By default, an instance of the email backend specified in\\n362:     :setting:`EMAIL_BACKEND` will be used.\\n363: \\n364:     The ``reporter_class`` argument of ``AdminEmailHandler`` allows providing\\n365:     an ``django.views.debug.ExceptionReporter`` subclass to customize the\\n366:     traceback text sent in the email body. The email subject will\\n325:     include the phrase \\\"internal IP\\\" if the client's IP address is in the\\n326:     :setting:`INTERNAL_IPS` setting; if not, it will include \\\"EXTERNAL IP\\\".\\n327: \\n328:     If the log record contains stack trace information, that stack\\n329:     trace will be included in the email.\\n330: \\n331:     The ``include_html`` argument of ``AdminEmailHandler`` is used to\\n332:     control whether the traceback email includes an HTML attachment\\n333:     containing the full content of the debug web page that would have been\\n334:     produced if :setting:`DEBUG` were ``True``. The migration to plain `[email, ...]` string lists (ticket #36138) affects **6 source files**, **5 test files**, and **5 documentation files**. The core processing logic lives in a single function (`_send_server_message` in `django/core/mail/__init__.py`) which validates the tuple format and extracts email addresses via `a[1]`.\n\n## Root Cause\n\nThe tuple format is consumed by a single core function that validates and unpacks it:\n\n**`django/core/mail/__init__.py:122-147`** — `_send_server_message()`\n```python\ndef _send_server_message(*, setting_name, subject, message, html_message=None,\n                         fail_silently=False, connection=None):\n    recipients = getattr(settings, setting_name)\n    if not recipients:\n        return\n    if not all(isinstance(a, (list, tuple)) and len(a) == 2 for a in recipients):\n        raise ValueError(f\"The {setting_name} setting must be a list of 2-tuples.\")\n    mail = EmailMultiAlternatives(\n        subject=\"%s%s\" % (settings.EMAIL_SUBJECT_PREFIX, subject),\n        body=message,\n        from_email=settings.SERVER_EMAIL,\n        to=[a[1] for a in recipients],   # <-- extracts email from tuple\n        connection=connection,\n    )\n```\n\nBoth `mail_admins()` and `mail_managers()` delegate to this function, passing `setting_name=\"ADMINS\"` or `setting_name=\"MANAGERS\"` respectively.\n\n## Evidence\n\n### 1. Files That Read `settings.ADMINS` or `settings.MANAGERS`\n\n| File | Line(s) | How It Reads the Setting |\n|------|---------|--------------------------|\n| `django/core/mail/__init__.py` | 131 | `getattr(settings, setting_name)` — reads ADMINS or MANAGERS dynamically |\n| `django/core/mail/__init__.py` | 135 | Validates each item is a 2-tuple: `isinstance(a, (list, tuple)) and len(a) == 2` |\n| `django/core/mail/__init__.py` | 142 | Unpacks tuple to get email: `to=[a[1] for a in recipients]` |\n| `django/utils/log.py` | 97 | `not settings.ADMINS` — truthiness check for early return in `AdminEmailHandler.emit()` |\n| `django/utils/log.py` | 138 | `mail.mail_admins(...)` — calls through to `_send_server_message` |\n| `django/middleware/common.py` | 131 | `mail_managers(...)` in `BrokenLinkEmailsMiddleware.process_response()` — indirect consumer |\n| `django/core/management/commands/sendtestemail.py` | 43-46 | `mail_managers(...)` and `mail_admins(...)` — indirect consumers |\n| `django/conf/global_settings.py` | 26 | `ADMINS = []` — default value definition |\n| `django/conf/global_settings.py` | 174 | `MANAGERS = ADMINS` — default value definition |\n\n### 2. Files That Validate or Type-Check the Settings Format\n\n| File | Line(s) | Validation |\n|------|---------|------------|\n| `django/core/mail/__init__.py` | 135-136 | `if not all(isinstance(a, (list, tuple)) and len(a) == 2 for a in recipients): raise ValueError(...)` |\n\nThis is the **only** validation point. It enforces the 2-tuple structure and raises `ValueError` if the format is wrong.\n\n### 3. Files That Unpack the Tuple Format\n\n| File | Line(s) | Unpacking Pattern |\n|------|---------|-------------------|\n| `django/core/mail/__init__.py` | 142 | `[a[1] for a in recipients]` — extracts email (index 1) from each tuple |\n| `tests/mail/tests.py` | 1802 | `for _, address in value` — unpacks name and email in test assertions |\n\n### 4. `ADMINS=[(\"Admin\", \"admin@fattie-breakie.com\")]` |\n| `tests/middleware/tests.py` | 392 | `MANAGERS=[(\"PHD\", \"PHB@dilbert.com\")]` |\n\n## Affected Components\n\n1. **`django.core.mail`** — Core processing: `_send_server_message()`, `mail_admins()`, `mail_managers()`\n2. **`django.utils.log`** — `AdminEmailHandler` reads `settings.ADMINS` for early-return check and delegates to `mail_admins()`\n3. **`django.middleware.common`** — `BrokenLinkEmailsMiddleware` calls `mail_managers()` for 404 notifications\n4. **`django.core.management.commands.sendtestemail`** — Management command calls `mail_admins()`/`mail_managers()`\n5. **`django.conf.global_settings`** — Default values and format documentation comments\n6. **Test suites** — `tests/mail/`, `tests/logging_tests/`, `tests/view_tests/`, `tests/middleware/`\n7. **Documentation** — `docs/ref/settings.txt`, `docs/howto/error-reporting.txt`, `docs/topics/email.txt`, `docs/ref/logging.txt`, `docs/howto/deployment/checklist.txt`\n\n## Third-Party Compatibility Concerns\n\n1. **All existing Django projects** that have `ADMINS` or `MANAGERS` configured in their `settings.py` use the tuple format (e.g., `ADMINS = [(\"Admin Name\", \"admin@example.com\")]`). The migration will break their configuration unless a deprecation shim is provided.\n\n2. **Third-party packages** that call `mail_admins()` or `mail_managers()` are **not affected** since they don't directly consume the setting — they go through the Django API. However, packages that directly read `settings.ADMINS` or `settings.MANAGERS` and unpack tuples (e.g., `for name, email in settings.ADMINS`) will break.\n\n3. **The `name` field is discarded**: Currently, the name portion of the tuple is completely ignored by `_send_server_message()` (it only uses `a[1]`). Users who relied on the name for display purposes elsewhere will lose that data.\n\n4. **Backward compatibility**: The Sourcegraph version of the code (default branch) already has the deprecation shim in place — it checks for the old format, issues a `RemovedInDjango70Warning`, and extracts emails from tuples. The local checkout is the pre-migration state.\n\n## Recommendation\n\n### Migration Checklist\n\nEach file that needs changes, ordered by priority:\n\n#### Core Source Files (must change)\n\n| # | File | Line(s) | Change Needed |\n|---|------|---------|---------------|\n| 1 | `django/core/mail/__init__.py` | 122-147 | **Primary change**: Modify `_send_server_message()` to accept plain email strings instead of 2-tuples. Replace validation at line 135 to check for list-of-strings format. Replace `to=[a[1] for a in recipients]` at line 142 with `to=recipients`. Add deprecation shim for old tuple format with `RemovedInDjango70Warning`. |\n| 2 | `django/conf/global_settings.py` | 24-25 | Update comment from `# [('Full Name', 'email@example.com'), ...]` to `# [\"email@example.com\", ...]` |\n| 3 | `django/conf/global_settings.py` | 172-174 | Update comment for MANAGERS if needed |\n\n#### Documentation Files (must update)\n\n| # | File | Line(s) | Change Needed |\n|---|------|---------|---------------|\n| 4 | `docs/ref/settings.txt` | 55-57 | Change example from `[(\"John\", \"john@example.com\"), (\"Mary\", \"mary@example.com\")]` to `[\"john@example.com\", '\"Mary\" <mary@example.com>']`. Add `versionchanged:: 6.0` note. |\n| 5 | `docs/ref/settings.txt` | 2073 | Update MANAGERS description if the \"same format as ADMINS\" reference needs clarification |\n| 6 | `docs/internals/deprecation.txt` | 34-35 | Add entry for Django 7.0 removal of tuple support |\n| 7 | `docs/releases/6.0.txt` | (new) | Add deprecation note for tuple format in the 6.0 release notes |\n\n#### Test Files (must update to match new format)\n\n| # | File | Line(s) | Change Needed |\n|---|------|---------|---------------|\n| 8 | `tests/mail/tests.py` | 1142 | Change `ADMINS=[(\"nobody\", \"nobody@example.com\")]` to `ADMINS=[\"nobody@example.com\"]` |\n| 9 | `tests/mail/tests.py` | 1155 | Change `MANAGERS=[(\"nobody\", \"nobody@example.com\")]` to `MANAGERS=[\"nobody@example.com\"]` |\n| 10 | `tests/mail/tests.py` | 1780-1803 | Rewrite `test_mail_admins_and_managers` to test with new string format. Add separate deprecation test for old tuple format. |\n| 11 | `tests/mail/tests.py` | 1805 | Update `MANAGERS` fixture to string format |\n| 12 | `tests/mail/tests.py` | 1820 | Update `ADMINS` fixture to string format |\n| 13 | `tests/mail/tests.py` | 1836-1837 | Update both `ADMINS` and `MANAGERS` fixtures to string format |\n| 14 | `tests/mail/tests.py` | 1862-1884 | Update `test_wrong_admins_managers` — adjust which formats are considered invalid (plain lists of strings should now be valid, tuples should trigger deprecation) |\n| 15 | `tests/mail/test_sendtestemail.py` | 7-14 | Change from tuple format to string format |\n| 16 | `tests/logging_tests/tests.py` | 251, 283, 322, 344, 375, 438, 452, 570 | Change all `ADMINS=[(\"name\", \"email\")]` to `ADMINS=[\"email\"]` (8 occurrences) |\n| 17 | `tests/view_tests/tests/test_debug.py` | 1454, 1490, 1533 | Change `ADMINS=[(\"Admin\", \"admin@fattie-breakie.com\")]` to `ADMINS=[\"admin@fattie-breakie.com\"]` (3 occurrences) |\n| 18 | `tests/middleware/tests.py` | 392 | Change `MANAGERS=[(\"PHD\", \"PHB@dilbert.com\")]` to `MANAGERS=[\"PHB@dilbert.com\"]` |\n\n#### New Tests to Add\n\n| # | File | Change Needed |\n|---|------|---------------|\n| 19 | `tests/mail/tests.py` | Add test for deprecation warning when using old tuple format |\n| 20 | `tests/mail/tests.py` | Add test that old tuple format still works (with deprecation warning) during transition period |\n| 21 | `tests/mail/tests.py` | Add test that new string format works correctly |\n| 22 | `tests/mail/tests.py` | Add test for `ImproperlyConfigured` (or `ValueError`) when format is completely invalid |\n\n### Migration Strategy\n\n1. **Phase 1 (Django 6.0)**: Add deprecation shim — detect old tuple format, issue `RemovedInDjango70Warning`, extract emails. New string format becomes the documented default.\n2. **Phase 2 (Django 7.0)**: Remove deprecation shim. Only string format accepted. Core processing: `_send_server_message()`, `mail_admins()`, `mail_managers()`\n2. **`django.utils.log`** — `AdminEmailHandler` reads `settings.ADMINS` for early-return check and delegates to `mail_admins()`\n3. **`django.middleware.common`** — `BrokenLinkEmailsMiddleware` calls `mail_managers()` for 404 notifications\n4. **`django.core.management.commands.sendtestemail`** — Management command calls `mail_admins()`/`mail_managers()`\n5. **`django.conf.global_settings`** — Default values and format documentation comments\n6. **Test suites** — `tests/mail/`, `tests/logging_tests/`, `tests/view_tests/`, `tests/middleware/`\n7. **Documentation** — `docs/ref/settings.txt`, `docs/howto/error-reporting.txt`, `docs/topics/email.txt`, `docs/ref/logging.txt`, `docs/howto/deployment/checklist.txt`\n\n## Third-Party Compatibility Concerns\n\n1. **All existing Django projects** that have `ADMINS` or `MANAGERS` configured in their `settings.py` use the tuple format (e.g., `ADMINS = [(\"Admin Name\", \"admin@example.com\")]`). The migration will break their configuration unless a deprecation shim is provided.\n\n2. **Third-party packages** that call `mail_admins()` or `mail_managers()` are **not affected** since they don't directly consume the setting — they go through the Django API. However, packages that directly read `settings.ADMINS` or `settings.MANAGERS` and unpack tuples (e.g., `for name, email in settings.ADMINS`) will break.\n\n3. **The `name` field is discarded**: Currently, the name portion of the tuple is completely ignored by `_send_server_message()` (it only uses `a[1]`). Users who relied on the name for display purposes elsewhere will lose that data.\n\n4. **Backward compatibility**: The Sourcegraph version of the code (default branch) already has the deprecation shim in place — it checks for the old format, issues a `RemovedInDjango70Warning`, and extracts emails from tuples. The local checkout is the pre-migration state.\n\n## Recommendation\n\n### Migration Checklist\n\nEach file that needs changes, ordered by priority:\n\n#### Core Source Files (must change)\n\n| # | File | Line(s) | Change Needed |\n|---|------|---------|---------------|\n| 1 | `django/core/mail/__init__.py` | 122-147 | **Primary change**: Modify `_send_server_message()` to accept plain email strings instead of 2-tuples. Replace validation at line 135 to check for list-of-strings format. Replace `to=[a[1] for a in recipients]` at line 142 with `to=recipients`. Add deprecation shim for old tuple format with `RemovedInDjango70Warning`. |\n| 2 | `django/conf/global_settings.py` | 24-25 | Update comment from `# [('Full Name', 'email@example.com'), ...]` to `# [\"email@example.com\", ...]` |\n| 3 | `django/conf/global_settings.py` | 172-174 | Update comment for MANAGERS if needed |\n\n#### Documentation Files (must update)\n\n| # | File | Line(s) | Change Needed |\n|---|------|---------|---------------|\n| 4 | `docs/ref/settings.txt` | 55-57 | Change example from `[(\"John\", \"john@example.com\"), (\"Mary\", \"mary@example.com\")]` to `[\"john@example.com\", '\"Mary\" <mary@example.com>']`. Add `versionchanged:: 6.0` note. |\n| 5 | `docs/ref/settings.txt` | 2073 | Update MANAGERS description if the \"same format as ADMINS\" reference needs clarification |\n| 6 | `docs/internals/deprecation.txt` | 34-35 | Add entry for Django 7.0 removal of tuple support |\n| 7 | `docs/releases/6.0.txt` | (new) | Add deprecation note for tuple format in the 6.0 release notes |\n\n#### Test Files (must update to match new format)\n\n| # | File | Line(s) | Change Needed |\n|---|------|---------|---------------|\n| 8 | `tests/mail/tests.py` | 1142 | Change `ADMINS=[(\"nobody\", \"nobody@example.com\")]` to `ADMINS=[\"nobody@example.com\"]` |\n| 9 | `tests/mail/tests.py` | 1155 | Change `MANAGERS=[(\"nobody\", \"nobody@example.com\")]` to `MANAGERS=[\"nobody@example.com\"]` |\n| 10 | `tests/mail/tests.py` | 1780-1803 | Rewrite `test_mail_admins_and_managers` to test with new string format.
